- **IPC.** `agent-usage:read` (main window, `mainOnly`; `usageIpc.ts`) and `agent-usage:changed` carry `UsageEntry[]` (`usageTypes.ts`): a reading or a `UsageBlock` with just what the Settings > Agents row is worded from. No connection ID, profile, account, token or diagnostic crosses.

**Sign-out and replacement.**
- A cancelled or failed replacement leaves the stored connection and its verified state as they were, unless the live login was touched (a keychain provider's login exited 0 in the live profile, or a vault provider's commit failed and the previous vault image could not be restored).
- Codex replacements are staged in `profiles/codex-<id>/` because `codex login` revokes and deletes the profile's existing login before its login server starts; the old profile is logged out only after success.
- With conversation runs active (an idle Codex app-server counts; model list and usage reads and a Claude run left with only a done turn's background work, whose lease is quiet, do not), sign-out and replacement return `stop_required` unless sent with `stop: true`, which stops those runs and waits for their leases.
- Sign-out succeeds only when a fresh status process reports the profile signed out; a failure persists in `signOutFailed` and blocks runs and sign-ins across restarts. Sign-out is local; history stays, and another account never runs the earlier conversations: a send to such a conversation refuses with `workspace_changed` when only the Claude Code organization differs (`workspace_mismatch` with both organization ids known, `organizationsKnown`), with `account_changed` for another account or another workspace otherwise (`account_mismatch`, `workspace_mismatch` with an organization id missing, and GitHub Copilot's `host`), which signing in to that account and workspace again brings back, and with `sign_in_changed` for a sign-in that cannot be proven the same or another Doan profile (`account_unverifiable`, `profile_mismatch`, or a stored session in a profile a staged replacement left; `judgmentCode` and the `configDir` check in `prepare.ts`).

**Browser links.** A link reaches only the owning window; `open-link` opens main's latest link for the attempt, and a window can never name a URL.
- Every Claude and Antigravity process, runs included, gets `BROWSER=<userData>/agent-auth/browser-hook`, which prints the URL instead of opening a browser; without a usable hook the CLI would open the default browser, so nothing starts. The agent's tool shell inherits it, so a command it runs to open a browser opens nothing.
- Claude: main forwards only the hook's authorize URL (`response_type=code`, localhost `/callback`), never the printed manual-code URL, which Doan has no entry for. A Claude `browser` setting overrides `BROWSER` and makes the sign-in fail.
- Antigravity: only a Google auth link returning to `127.0.0.1`; `submit-code` delivers only an address for the same listener and state. Logs keep Doan's own categories only, because the server's messages carry the link, the account and credential fields. Status and logout limits start at the `initialize` answer: a slow server start would otherwise fail sign-ins the user already approved.
- `codex login`, `cursor-agent login` and `copilot login` open the system browser themselves; Doan only offers Open again (accepted; BACKLOG.md).

**IPC.** `agent-auth:*` (types in `agentAuth/ipc.ts`), main window's main frame only (`senderAllowed.ts`); Settings > Agents never touches conversations (§7 'Settings IPC'). `changed` carries the whole snapshot with a revision and `onAgentAuth` drops older revisions, so a racing initial read never replaces newer state. Main-only `AuthIpc.check(provider)` also returns the outcome (`checkVerdict`); the window's reply does not.

**GitHub token exception.** Copilot is the only place Doan holds a GitHub token (exception to §9): the official Copilot login's token, whose broad scopes (`read:user read:org repo gist codespace write:plugin_gateway_connections`) Doan cannot narrow. It stays encrypted in the vault, is materialized only into the Copilot HOME for Copilot processes, and is never used for repository access.

**Antigravity identity exception.** Only to show the email, Doan reads the client ID, secret and refresh token from its own working copy and makes one refresh grant to `https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token` and one userinfo `GET`. The file's `token_uri` must equal that address and its `scopes` include `userinfo.email`, or nothing is sent; redirects are refused. The access token stays in memory and never reaches state, metadata, logs or a window; Doan never writes the credential file. Widening this needs its own decision.

**Cursor usage token exception.** Only to read usage, Doan reads the access token from the Cursor lease's working copy (a regular file, no link, size-capped) and sends it as the dashboard session cookie in one `GET https://cursor.com/api/usage-summary` (`usageRead.ts`). The host is fixed, redirects and answers from another origin are refused, and the answer is capped in size and time. The token stays in memory for that call; it is never refreshed (an expired one is `read_failed`) and never reaches state, metadata, logs, IPC, error text or a window. The API is private, so any other shape or failure is `read_failed`. Widening this needs its own decision.

**Known limits.**
- Real token expiry, refresh and concurrent refresh have not been observed for any provider.
- Cursor's login shell puts system paths first on PATH, so its tool shell may not run the bundled `doan` first.
- Accepted risks of the minimum-version policy: changed meaning under unchanged shapes is not caught; text-based classifications can fall back to generic codes; Claude incompatibility is never recorded; an incompatible provider looks ready until its first run. Cursor and Copilot self-update; Copilot ACP is a preview.
- Tool-fact probes run provider CLIs under the real HOME and write under `~/.codex` and `~/.local/share/cursor-agent`.
- Cursor does not ask before editing inside the workspace. Several Codex app-servers on one `CODEX_HOME` (a conversation, the check and the lookup) may refresh the same keychain token.
- A SIGKILL during a staged Codex replacement leaves its login process running; the sweep removes only the profile. A successful sign-out leaves a staged profile directory, minus its credential.
- Claude Code's and Codex's keychain items are keyed by the profile path and stay after userData is deleted; signing out first removes them. `doan Safe Storage` (the vault key) and MCP connector tokens are keychain items too; whether they stay was not checked. A sign-out that answered `stop_required` and was never confirmed is cancelled by quit: the connection stays and shows as connected on the next launch, so the person can sign out again.
- Antigravity: in the runs checked (sign-in, status, one turn, sign-out), Doan's server and its language server wrote neither the real `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/` nor the `gemini | antigravity` keychain item with the environment above. Writes seen there come from the person's own `agy`, which other apps (Orca) run on a schedule. The language server's uses of `go-keyring` and `os/user.Current` were not ruled out statically.
- Fence ('Fence' above), all providers:
  - Other processes of the same user (a terminal agent, Orca) can read userData: file permissions do not separate one uid.
  - It holds only direct access inside the run. Settings the agent leaves elsewhere (`~/.zshrc`, a repository's `.claude/settings.json` hooks, `.mcp.json`) can run later outside it, and a model list read runs outside it with the repository's settings, so repository hooks may run then.
  - `doan check` inside it cannot add a session association to `<userData>/sessions.jsonl`; its output and exit code are unchanged.
  - `sandbox-exec` is marked deprecated in its man page; if macOS removes it, outer-fenced runs fail to start.
- Fence, Claude:
  - Doan does not read MDM or server-delivered managed settings. If they turn the sandbox on, Doan judges it off and every Bash command fails with exit 71 inside the outer fence; if they turn it off, Doan judges it on and only `permissions.deny` remains, which does not hold a program that opens files itself (`perl`).
  - On a sandbox-on turn, hooks and MCP servers named by the repository's Claude settings (`.claude/` settings, skills, agents and skills-dir plugins, `.mcp.json`, whose servers run without approval under `enableAllProjectMcpServers`) run outside every fence. The agent can write them on a sandbox-off turn, which also switches the next turn to sandbox-on (in Full access without any approval).
  - A sandbox-on turn cannot run a command outside Claude's sandbox. A userData entry made during the turn is missing from the Read and Edit tools' deny list (Bash still cannot read it).
  - A pasted image sent with Send now during a fenced turn was not opened for that turn, so the message waits in the queue for the next one.
- Fence, Codex:
  - Sandboxed commands in Ask first, Plan and Accept edits reach the network without approval, and those modes do not start while the app has no runtime (no socket).
  - The denied entries are listed when the server starts, so a userData entry made later (another provider signed in meanwhile, a new staging profile) is open until that server ends. Sandboxed and approved escalated commands can list userData's entry names; a denied entry's content stays closed.
  - An approved escalated command can write userData outside the denied entries (`runtime.json`, attachments, new entries) and the profile's `skills/` and `plugins/`, doan stub included; MCP dependencies and hooks it plants there run outside the sandbox on a later profile server. The approval card shows the command. One probe saw an approved `apply_patch` fail to write a denied entry (`conversations/`); the real app did not check it.
  - Permission profiles and `turn/start` `permissions` are an experimental API. Ask first's sandbox is Doan's read-only profile, so a `sandbox_mode` or `default_permissions` written into Doan's Codex profile `config.toml` is unused.
  - A server that runs only in Full access cannot create or update `skills/.system` (Codex's built-in skills), and a Full access turn was seen without the plugin skills the same conversation listed in Ask first (cause unconfirmed).

### MCP connectors

Entry: `app/src/main/mcpConnectors/` (`service.ts`; pure rules in the other modules, each with its test). Product rules: PRODUCT §3 In scope 9 and 'MCP tools', §5 item 0 'MCP Connectors'. Claude Code and Codex only; ACP runners still open sessions with `mcpServers: []`.

- **Ownership.** Doan keeps the server list. The provider CLI registers a server in the Doan profile, runs its MCP OAuth sign-in with its own client, and keeps the token in the OS keychain, so Doan still ships no OAuth client ('Agent authentication'). Doan never reads, writes or deletes a token or keychain item; tokens go only through the provider's `mcp logout` and `mcp remove`. Commands are `COMMANDS` in `service.ts`, run with the provider's command, base arguments and `providerEnv` like any agent-auth process.
- **Server names.** Doan registers every server as `doan-<slug>` (`CONNECTOR_NAME`, `connectorName` in `names.ts`): fixed slugs for the presets (`presets.ts`), and for a URL server a slug of its host, at most 16 characters, with `-2`, `-3` when taken. Codex keys an MCP token's keychain account as `<name>|<hash of the URL>`, so a Doan server named like the person's terminal server at the same URL would share its token, and Doan's Disconnect, Remove or sign-out would delete the terminal's. Claude keeps MCP tokens inside the profile's keychain item and cannot collide, but follows the same rule. Every cleanup (reconcile, sign-out logouts) touches only `doan-*` names. Tools reach agents as `mcp__doan-<slug>__<tool>` (Claude) and `mcp__doan_<slug>__<tool>` (Codex).
- **URLs.** `parseConnectorUrl` normalizes input once, and the stored form is that URL's `href`. A stored URL is never normalized again (dropping one trailing slash is not idempotent): the same string is registered, recorded in a pending logout and written into a staging `config.toml`, because Codex derives the keychain key from it.
- **Leases and attempts.** Every MCP command holds the profile through `acquire(provider, "mcp", added)` (`agentAuth/manager.ts`); `added` carries the Claude sign-in's link-file environment (`hookLinks`). Like a model list read, an `"mcp"` lease never counts toward `stop_required` (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-30). Each provider runs one attempt (Connect, Disconnect or Remove) at a time, in a per-provider queue; Remove spans both providers and starts only while neither runs one. A sign-in or sign-out write first stops that provider's MCP work through `stopMcp`, separately from `stopRuns`, because a pending Codex logout runs in a temporary home and holds no lease. Quit stops every attempt, and a stopped attempt writes nothing.
- **Claude sign-in.** `claude mcp login` refuses to run without a terminal, so it runs in a node-pty (`pty.ts`). The pty's child leads its own process group (forkpty's `setsid`, pinned by `pty.test.ts`) and the whole group is stopped with `OwnedGroup`, since node-pty's `kill()` signals one pid ('Quit and process cleanup'). The link comes only from the `BROWSER` hook's link file, never from the pty output, which carries terminal control sequences and serves as diagnostics. `mcpAuthLink` accepts an `https` PKCE code request (`response_type=code`, a `code_challenge`) returning to `localhost` or `127.0.0.1` `/callback` on a port from 1024 to 65535, on any host, because each server authorizes on its own host. Limits (`LIMITS` in `service.ts`): the link must arrive within 30 s of the login's start (a Claude `browser` setting overrides `BROWSER`, and no link comes), and approval within 5 minutes of the link.
- **Codex sign-in.** `codex mcp add` registers and starts the sign-in, or `codex mcp login` for a server already registered. Codex opens the default browser itself, so Doan opens no link (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-30 'Doan opened a second Codex sign-in page'). The approval limit is the same 5 minutes.
- **Token storage.** The Codex base arguments (`agentAuth/providers.ts`) add `mcp_oauth_credentials_store="keyring"` to every Codex process, conversation servers included, for the reason `cli_auth_credentials_store` is there: `auto` can fall back to a file silently, and a token refresh during a conversation also saves. Codex therefore gets no plaintext check. After every Claude login, however it ended, a `.credentials.json` in the profile is deleted (`plaintextLeftovers`), and it fails an otherwise successful login as `storage_failed`.
- **State.** `connectorState` (`state.ts`) derives an item's state from readiness, the running attempt, the last status read, `everConnected` and a pending-logout block. Reconnect needed is not stored: it is "registered, not signed in" for a server whose `everConnected` holds that provider. A successful Connect adds the provider and a successful Disconnect removes it. While a pending logout blocks a server, a status that reads signed in shows as Reconnect needed, because the token may be the previous account's. Status commands use the network (Claude `mcp get` health-checks the server), so `refresh` runs them only when the page opens, after an attempt ends, on Retry, after a server is added and when the provider's sign-in changes, and never polls; a read that started before a success is discarded.
- **Registration.** A server registers at its first Connect with that provider, and a cancelled or failed first Connect keeps the registration (Codex's `mcp add` leaves one; the next Connect uses `mcp login`). Remove logs out, then removes, at every provider with a Doan connection, and only then drops the server from the file: Codex rebuilds the keychain key from the registered URL, so removing first would leave the token. Each time a provider becomes ready (app start, sign-in, replacement commit), it retries its pending logouts and reconciles: `reconcilePlan` logs out and removes the `doan-*` registrations that are neither a preset nor in the file (a Remove while that provider had no connection). An unreadable file reconciles nothing, and a missing registration is never added.
- **Files** (`files.ts`, `store.ts`; `files.test.ts` and `store.test.ts` are their schemas). Both are rewritten whole through an exclusive 0600 temporary file and a rename.
  - `<userData>/mcp-connectors.json` (`v: 1`): the URL servers and `everConnected`. An unreadable file is not read as defaults, because the providers keep Doan's registrations and an empty list would drift from them: it stays in place, refuses every write, and Add and Remove are refused, while presets still connect. A newer `v` is never read or written.
  - `<userData>/mcp-connectors-pending.json` (`v: 1`): the logouts that failed during a sign-out or replacement (provider, name, registered URL). It is separate so that blocking works while the connectors file is unreadable. A record that cannot be written, or whose removal cannot be, blocks that server's Connect in memory until quit. An unreadable file is moved aside to `.broken-<time>`; while the file was unreadable or newer, every `doan-*` server stays blocked for both providers until one logout of it succeeds in this run, since the lost records cannot be known.
- **Sign-out and replacement boundary.** agentAuth calls the injected `mcpLogout(provider, request)` (`McpLogoutRequest` in `agentAuth/manager.ts`) inside a sign-out and after a committed replacement, and `McpService.logoutForAuth` implements it; agentAuth knows no connector file, and the step never fails the sign-out. Targets come from the provider's own list (`claude mcp list`, `codex mcp list --json`) rather than the file, so a broken file stops no logout; a server the list shows signed out is skipped. When the list cannot be read, the targets are the union of the `doan-*` servers in the profile's own config (`profileConfig.ts`, names and URLs only) and every server Doan knows, so a config parser miss never drops a known server. The plan is `mcpLogoutPlan` and `mcpLogoutOutcome` (`plans.ts`):
  - Codex sign-out logs out each server before the provider logout, which does not clear MCP keychain entries; a server whose logout failed is removed from the live profile and recorded.
  - Claude sign-out adds no step, because `claude auth logout` clears the profile's MCP tokens, and it clears Claude's pending records.
  - Replacement runs only after the commit (a replacement that does not finish leaves registrations and tokens, POSTMORTEM 2026-10-29), and the provider admits nothing until it ends. A replacement keeping the stored account ID logs nothing out; Codex stores none, so each of its replacements logs out. Claude logs out the live profile's servers. Codex logs out in the previous profile, then appends the logged-out servers (and those listed signed out) to the staging profile's `config.toml` with `codexServerToml`, because `codex mcp add` would start a new OAuth sign-in and open the browser even with a token present.
  - Pending logouts are retried when a provider becomes ready and right before that server's Connect, which proceeds only after success. Codex retries at app start whether or not it is signed in, in a temporary `CODEX_HOME` under the OS temporary directory whose `config.toml` holds only that server: the keychain key depends on the name and URL alone, and the old profile may be gone.
- **Codex turns.** Before every doan-owned turn the runner waits until every `doan-*` server in the profile's `config.toml` (`codexProfileServers`, `conversation/mcpStartup.ts`) reported a status other than `starting` in `mcpServer/startupStatus/updated` for its thread, because a turn that starts earlier runs without that server. The notification has a `threadId` and no `turnId`, so the runner takes it by thread instead of the turn's transport. Past 1 s an `activity` (`kind: "other"`) says "Waiting for …", and at 10 s (`startupGate.ts`) a second one says which servers were not ready and the turn starts; no new event type exists, so the history format is unchanged. History is append-only, so the renderer (`threadItems.ts`) folds a Waiting line followed by the same servers' not-ready line into one.
  - Each provider has a connector generation (`McpService.generation`) that a successful Connect, Disconnect or Remove raises; a replacement commit does not, because a Codex replacement opens new runners on its new profile. A runner remembers the generation it read its servers at; before a turn, when the generation changed, it forgets those servers' statuses and sends `config/mcpServer/reload`, without restarting the server. A newly opened runner sends none, a refused reload is sent again before the next turn, and a running turn keeps its tools.
  - Claude starts its SDK on every turn with the `user` setting source, which reads the profile's servers, so it needs neither wait nor reload.
- **Codex MCP approvals** (`codexApproval.ts`, wired in `conversation/transport.ts` `codexPermission`). Before a tool without a read-only hint Codex sends `mcpServer/elicitation/request` with `_meta.codex_approval_kind: "mcp_tool_call"`; it becomes a `permission.request` with tool `mcpToolCall`. Allow answers `{action: "accept", content: {}}` and Deny `{action: "decline"}`; Allow for this session (with `_meta.persist: "session"`) appears only when `_meta.persist` offers `session`. `always` is never offered, because it writes a lasting approval into the provider's config. The tool name is read from `message` (fallback "Run a tool on <server>"), and the server is named from `connectorLabels`, passed through `CodexContext.labels` (`dispatcher.begin`), so a `doan-*` name never shows; other servers keep Codex's name. Any other elicitation, and an approval with no running turn to ask in or arriving after Stop, is declined at once, so the call fails and the turn goes on instead of ending as `session_unavailable` (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-27 'Codex ingress confused unowned requests and notification errors').
- **Claude MCP permissions.** The SDK's `canUseTool` already covers MCP tools; `claudePermissionSummary` reads `mcp__doan-<slug>__<tool>` as "Run <tool> on <name>", and an MCP tool's input never reaches a summary, activity or the history.
- **IPC.** `mcp-connectors:*` invoke channels (`mcpConnectors/ipc.ts`), main window's main frame only, listed in `senderAllowed.ts`; main sends `mcp-connectors:changed` with the whole snapshot, a revision and each provider's generation. `open-link` reopens only main's latest link for the attempt. The renderer imports only `presets.ts` by value (types from the others); `presets.ts` and `names.ts`, which renderer tests import, stay free of Node modules (Settings IPC 'Renderer import boundary').

### Design preset reads

The app reads the CLI's design presets (§6, §12) for the composers' preset pickers; the renderer keeps no catalog copy. Entry: `app/src/main/designSkills.ts` (`designSkills.test.ts`).

- **Channels and callers.** `design-skills:list|get` are `mainOnly` (`senderAllowed.ts`); other senders and malformed arguments get `request_refused` and no CLI runs. The app does not judge the id; the CLI's `usage_error` comes back.
- **Execution.** One run of the CLI `cliCommand` selects ('CLI lookup and local package' above), id after `--`, no cwd or repo, a timeout, no cache or retry. A spawn error is `cli_error`/`unavailable`, never a rejected promise. No model, conversation or file write.
- **Interpretation**: app-side failures are `cli_error` reasons; any other `ok: false` passes the CLI's envelope unchanged. `get` requires the requested id and the body's end marker line, so a cut body is never returned. Success keeps only contract fields; a missing `group` or another `result.version` is still success. A failure is never an empty list. App failures have empty `nextSteps`; `message` is display text, not contract.
- **Telling an older CLI.** A `list()` `usage_error` means no `design-skill` command; a `get(id)` one after a successful `list()` is an unknown id. stderr is never read for meaning; its first line is only quoted in the failure when the CLI exits non-zero without JSON on stdout.
- **Examples.** `schema/fixtures/cli-envelope/design-skill/`.
- **Conversations.** `PRESET_GUIDANCE` (`conversation/prompt.ts`) is one fixed paragraph on every turn pointing at `doan skill`; main adds no list or body and no classifier, so the app cannot show which preset a request will map to.
- **Connecting a chosen preset.** A chosen preset is a fixed first line of the user's text (`composeRequest` in `app/src/renderer/homeComposer.ts`) that the guide's named-preset rule reads; there is no rewriting API. The UI cannot know whether a preset needs a selected frame, so the agent confirms the target (BACKLOG.md 'Undecided'). Project skills use the same mechanism: one fixed line per highlighted skill after the preset line. The line formats and their stripping live in `app/src/main/conversation/requestLines.ts`, which has no imports so the renderer and main's conversation title share it; display and titles strip the fixed-line block of every paragraph, because a combined queue joins composed requests.

## 8. CLI stack: Go native binary

- `doan` is a native Go binary for fast startup, shipped inside the app bundle (§7 'CLI lookup and local package'). The cost of two languages is that formats exist on both sides; §11 keeps them in one schema source.
- Dependencies are pinned in `go.mod`. `golang.org/x/net/html` parses canvases with the browser's HTML5 rules; `golang.org/x/sys/unix` provides the no-follow, descriptor-relative opens that session recording, the storage exclude file and export rely on (§4 'Shell check session associations', §12 'Storage'); `golang.org/x/text/unicode/norm` matches path spellings by NFC, as macOS does; santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema validates shared formats (§11).

## 9. Distribution

- **CI gate**: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs only the shared gate (`verify.mjs`, without `--eval`; AGENTS.md 'Commands'), no e2e, packaging or signing. It runs on pull requests to and pushes to `develop` and `main` on the pinned `macos-26` runner, and other workflows can call it with `workflow_call`. One run takes about 7 minutes (app-test about 5); the job timeout is about twice that.
  - The job id `gate` is the required check name for branch protection, so the job has no display name.
  - The workflow uses no secrets, reads with `permissions: contents: read` and never uses `pull_request_target`, because the repository is public and pull requests can come from forks. Actions are pinned to commit SHAs.
  - Cancelling an older run applies to pull requests only; on `develop` or `main` a cancelled run would leave a merged commit without a result.
  - Node is pinned in the root `.node-version`, which local runs and CI both read; steps such as `node --test 'app/runtime/*.test.ts'` load TypeScript directly and depend on the version. Go comes from `go.mod`.
- **Local package**: `npm --prefix app run package` builds the unsigned arm64 `Doan.app` (§7 'CLI lookup and local package'). It stays unsigned so eval, runtime probes and Codex sessions need no certificate, and their notification and keychain state does not change identity. It is a `--mac dir` build, so it has no update feed (below). A copy launched directly in an Applications folder runs agent setup against the launching HOME (§7 'Agent setup').
- **Signing in CI**: Developer ID signing and notarization run only in GitHub Actions, in the `sign` job of `.github/workflows/test-build.yml` (tester builds) and `release.yml` (releases). No machine's keychain is involved, and no encrypted key is ever committed, because the repository is public.
  - Both workflows start from a tag push: `tester-*` for a tester build, `v*` for a release, `release-dryrun-*` for a release build without the version check or the draft. `workflow_dispatch` is not used: it only starts when the workflow file is on the default branch, and `main` changes only at a release. A tag push runs the workflow file of the tagged commit.
  - Jobs: `source` (the tag commit must be on `origin/develop`, or `origin/main` for `v*`; releases also run `checkReleaseVersion.mjs --tag`), then the CI gate through `workflow_call`, then `sign`, and for `v*` only `publish`. The tag check is defence in depth: a tag's own workflow file runs, so the real guard is the environment's required reviewer.
  - The `release` environment holds the secrets and opens them only to `sign`: required reviewer = the maintainer; the maintainer approves tester builds and dry runs, while `/release` approves its own release run through the `pending_deployments` API after checking the run's head SHA is the tag commit, because invoking `/release` covers every approval; deployment refs = the tags `v*`, `tester-*` and `release-dryrun-*`. Secrets: `MAC_CERT_P12_BASE64` (the Developer ID Application certificate and key as a base64 `.p12`, passed as `CSC_LINK`), `MAC_CERT_PASSWORD` (`CSC_KEY_PASSWORD`), `APPLE_API_KEY_P8` (the App Store Connect API Team key's `.p8` content), `APPLE_API_KEY_ID`, `APPLE_API_ISSUER`; variable `MAC_IDENTITY_NAME` (`<Name> (<Team ID>)`, passed as `-c.mac.identity`). The first `sign` step checks they are set and that the issuer is a UUID, the key ID ten capitals or digits and the `.p8` a private key, printing only names, lengths and whether a value holds whitespace; a malformed ID otherwise fails only at notarization, after the app is signed. Agents never read, print or set them; the one-time setup and renewal belong to the `/release` skill.
  - electron-builder imports the `.p12` into its own temporary keychain on the runner; no intermediate certificate was added. `APPLE_API_KEY` must be a file path, so `sign` writes the `.p8` to `$RUNNER_TEMP` and removes it in an `if: always()` step. The dmg is notarized with `notarytool submit --key … --key-id … --issuer …`.
  - Signing is passed as command-line overrides (`-c.mac.identity`, `-c.dmg.sign=true`), so `app/package.json` keeps `mac.identity: null`. electron-builder signs every binary under `Contents/`, including `Resources/bin/doan` and node-pty's `pty.node`/`spawn-helper`. CI builds carry Team ID `7H4HNHTB45` and the same designated requirement as the earlier locally signed tester builds (`certificate leaf[subject.OU] = "7H4HNHTB45"`, not a cdhash), so installed copies keep their notification and keychain permissions across the move.
  - Entitlements: `app/resources/entitlements.mac.plist` holds only `allow-jit` and `allow-unsigned-executable-memory`, and `mac.entitlements` and `mac.entitlementsInherit` both point to it. Without `entitlementsInherit`, electron-builder signs helpers, the CLI and `spawn-helper` with its template, which adds `disable-library-validation`; Doan keeps library validation on because it holds agent credentials. Under these entitlements the window, node-pty and the `osascript` administrator path of agent setup were checked to work; Doan only spawns `/usr/bin/osascript`, so it needs no `automation.apple-events`.
  - Without notarization credentials electron-builder only warns, so `sign` requires "notarization successful" in its log. The dmg wait is capped at 30 minutes; a timeout prints the submission id, which `notarytool wait <id>` can follow. Measured on `macos-26`: `source` about 10 s, the gate about 7 min, `sign` 6.5 min for a tester build and 9.5 min for a release (app notarization 2.5–5 min, dmg about 1 min).
  - `app/scripts/verifySignedPackage.mjs <dmg> [<zip>]` checks the result the way a tester's Mac will: `codesign --verify --deep --strict`, a Developer ID authority, the runtime flag and a secure timestamp on every Mach-O, entitlements against the committed plist, the designated requirement, `stapler validate`, `spctl` for the app and for a quarantined dmg copy (electron-builder silently leaves the dmg unsigned when it cannot find the identity, and only this catches it), and with a zip the app inside it and the zip's `latest-mac.yml` entry. CI runs it before uploading; `/tester-build` and `/release` run it again on the downloaded files.
- **Tester builds**: `/tester-build` pushes `tester-<date>-<sha>` at `origin/develop`, and `test-build.yml` leaves `Doan-tester-<date>-<sha>.dmg` as an Actions artifact for 7 days. Tester builds have no update feed.
- **Release builds**: `/release` pushes `v<version>` at the merge commit on `main` (develop → main, or `hotfix/<slug>` → main for a hotfix). `release.yml` builds `--mac dmg zip`, then `publish` (no environment, no secrets, `contents: write`) checks the version again with a token that can see drafts and runs `gh release create v<version> --draft --verify-tag` (`--prerelease` for `-rc.N`) with no notes; `/release` checks the draft and, when the checks pass, adds the notes and publishes it. electron-builder never creates the tag (a draft published without one would get a tag on the default branch's HEAD), so the tag comes first.
  - Assets: `Doan-macos-arm64.dmg` (no version, so `releases/latest/download/Doan-macos-arm64.dmg` stays stable), `Doan-<version>-arm64-mac.zip`, its `.blockmap` and `latest-mac.yml`. The dmg is uploaded once, after stapling, because stapling changes its bytes; the dmg entry in `latest-mac.yml` keeps the pre-staple hash, which the macOS updater does not use (it downloads the zip, whose app is stapled).
  - `app/scripts/checkReleaseVersion.mjs <version>` (or `--tag v<version>`, which also requires `app/package.json` to match) accepts only `x.y.z` and `x.y.z-rc.N`, newer than the latest published stable release and than earlier tags of the same base, and fails with `bad_semver`, `tag_mismatch`, `published_exists`, `draft_exists` or `not_newer`. A tag alone, without a release, passes, so a failed run can be rerun; an existing draft must be deleted first.
- **Update feed**: only release builds contain `Contents/Resources/app-update.yml`. electron-builder writes it for every dmg or zip target, even with `--publish never`, and without a `publish` setting it derives a GitHub feed from the repository; only an explicit `publish: null` stops it, and the CLI cannot pass that (`-c.publish=null` arrives as the string `"null"`; only `mac.identity` is coerced). So `test-build.yml` builds with a generated config that copies `build` with `publish: null`, and the local `--mac dir` package never gets one. rc releases also write `latest-mac.yml`. The app treats `app-update.yml` as the feed switch (`hasFeedConfig`, §7 'App updates'): it reads `latest-mac.yml` from the release that `app-update.yml` names and downloads the zip, never the dmg. With `allowPrerelease`, electron-updater's GitHub provider (6.8.11, and unchanged through 7.0.0-alpha.13) takes a stable tag only for an `alpha` or `beta` copy, so on its own a `-rc.N` copy picks only the newest `-rc` tag; the app therefore also reads the stable release for a prerelease copy and takes the newer of the two (§7 'App updates'), so `x.y.z-rc.N` moves to a later rc or to a stable release. Copies at 0.1.0-rc.2 and earlier lack this and need a manual reinstall. A stable copy takes only the release GitHub's `/releases/latest` names. Updates replace the bundle in place, so `~/.local/share/doan/current` and the links through it keep pointing at the new CLI (§7 'Agent setup').
- **Notices**: the package carries the root `LICENSE` as `Contents/Resources/LICENSE` and `cli/internal/designskill/notices/` as `Contents/Resources/notices/` (`extraResources`).
- **App icon**: `mac.icon` is the Icon Composer bundle `app/resources/icon.icon` (white fill, one `doany.png` layer: the V2 artwork scaled to 72% of the 1024 canvas on its longer side, centred; shadow and translucency off). electron-builder compiles it with `actool` into `Contents/Resources/Assets.car` (`CFBundleIconName` `Icon`) and `icon.icns`, which also becomes the dmg volume icon. macOS 26 applies the icon shape and margins itself; a full-bleed PNG or icns is drawn shrunk on a backing plate once the app is registered in `/Applications`, so the icon stays a `.icon` bundle. `actool` comes with Xcode 26 or later (not the command line tools alone), so packaging needs it.
- **Installer disk**: `build.dmg` in `app/package.json` lays out the dmg: a white background (`app/resources/installer-background.png` 640 × 536, `@2x`) with the buddies' group photo at the top, an arrow between Doan (200, 306) and the Applications link (440, 306), and “A place for your next idea.” below them. The PNGs hold only the draft's background part; Finder draws the icons, labels, title and bars. electron-builder sizes the outer window from the background and ignores `dmg.window` when a background is set, and Finder's path and status bars cover the bottom of the content, so the artwork's text ends 105 px above the bottom edge; in an unsigned local dmg on macOS 26.3.1 the window content was 640 × 536 and the tagline sat clear of both bars (other macOS versions unchecked, BACKLOG). In Dark appearance Finder may draw the icon labels white on the white background; that is accepted, because the icons and the arrow carry the action. Draft: `.doan/canvases/buddy-installer.html` (`installer-row-tagline`).
- **Doan will be released as open source** under Apache-2.0 (root `LICENSE`).
- Design preset notices (`cli/internal/designskill/notices/`: Impeccable's `LICENSE` and `NOTICE.md` byte for byte, the ehmo/platform-design-skills MIT license, Doan's `MODIFICATIONS.md`) are embedded in the CLI and shipped as files in the package ('Notices' above). `SOURCES.md` keeps per-file provenance in the repo only.
- **Doan (app and CLI) does not handle GitHub tokens.** It neither reads nor stores them, except the Copilot connection's token held encrypted only for Copilot agent authentication (§7 'Agent authentication'). The agent does git and PRs with the user's credentials.

## 10. Moving between machines

- Doan never commits or pushes. With storage `repo`, canvases and `.doan/config.json` (versioned by `v`) move with `git pull`; with storage `local` they stay in the checkout that has them and move only as exported files (PRODUCT §5 item 0 'Doan files'); the app picks pulled canvases up through file watching. The read-state records in `.doan/seen/` move the same way, so a canvas whose HTML matches its record shows no dot on another machine or path (§7 'Seen canvases').
- Everything in userData, plus Doan's agent credentials, is machine-local and is not carried over (§11 'Device-local files'): a new machine redoes first run (agent sign-in, project choice; a pulled repo needs no setup), and opening Doan from Applications sets up the CLI and skills again (§7 'Agent setup').

## 11. Shared formats

- JSON Schema files in root `schema/` are the source of truth for every format both languages touch: `.doan/config.json`, the CLI envelope, `runtime.json`, `app-socket.v1`/`v2`/`v3`/`v4`/`v5`/`v6` and `sessions.v1`. TypeScript (ajv, `app/src/formats/validate.ts`) and Go (santhosh-tekuri/jsonschema v6, `cli/internal/formats/validate.go`) validate against the same files, draft 2020-12. `schema/embed.go` embeds them into Go; the Go module sits at the repo root because `go:embed` cannot reach parent directories.
  - Exception: each command's `result` has no schema; §12 states it and Go produces it, so two implementations cannot drift. `onboarding` passes on the app's v2 `result` and `active` the app's v1 selection without `root`, each validated against its socket schema first. The envelope schema keeps `result` as any value, and CLI integration tests validate the envelope, then deep-compare `result`.
- Shared fixtures in `schema/fixtures/` are read by both languages' tests.
- **Cross-file schemas**: each schema has an absolute `https://doan.design/schema/<name>.v<N>.schema.json` `$id`. Both languages register every schema before compiling; Go's external loader refuses network and disk fallback.
  - `app-socket.v1`'s root constrains nothing on purpose; callers must use the `$defs/request`, `$defs/response` or `$defs/selection` validator.
  - Socket objects reject unknown properties; numeric version normalization belongs to the readers, not the schema.
  - `runtime.json` is machine-local but shared-schema validated because TypeScript writes and Go reads it; `sessions.v1` likewise because Go writes and TypeScript reads. Filesystem facts (existence, canonical paths, profile equality, cwd containment) are checked outside the schema, and session IDs are opaque.
- **Onboarding, work, open, handoff and prototype sockets**: readers dispatch on numeric `v` before validating. Their fixtures live in `schema/fixtures/app-socket-v2/` through `schema/fixtures/app-socket-v6/`, not `app-socket/`, because v1-only readers glob that directory. v3 `session` follows the opaque session-ID contract of `sessions.v1`, and `session` and `provider` come together or not at all. v3 `update` carries `frame` (v4's `$defs/frameId`, referenced by its absolute `$id`), `note` and `conversation` (the same opaque-ID contract, never with `session` or `provider`), each only on `update`; `update` stayed in v3 without a new `v` (§4 'Work signals over app-socket v3'). v4's `frame` request field uses the deep link's bounds (non-empty, no control characters, at most 200 characters), its reply `frame` is `found`, `missing` or `none`, and `not_ready` is one of its error codes (§4). v5's `text` limit is in code points and sized for six-byte JSON escapes (Go `handoffTextLimit` and TypeScript `HANDOFF_TEXT_LIMIT` are pinned to it by tests that read the schema), and its `handoff` key pattern matches the CLI's own check (§4 'Handoff over app-socket v5'). v6's `frame` is v4's `$defs/frameId` by its absolute `$id` (code points, while the CLI and app also cap UTF-16 units; the fixtures hold an astral ID inside one bound and outside the other), its `mark` is convention 14's literal, and its reply codes carry no frame judgment (§4 'Playing a prototype over app-socket v6'). The `work`, `open`, `handoff` and `export` results have no schema (§12). The onboarding envelope mapping is data in `schema/fixtures/cli-envelope/onboarding/mapping.json`, read by Go, TypeScript and the CLI translator test; envelope tests glob only `valid/` and `invalid/` there, so other subdirectories are not read as envelopes.
- **Product-name fixtures**: `schema/fixtures/brand/cases.json` classifies each `doan`/`Doan` occurrence; app and CLI string tests fail on a lowercase `doan` in prose. Test data, not a format.
- **Open-link fixtures**: `schema/fixtures/open-link/cases.json` holds links Go must build (root, path, expected URL) and URLs TypeScript must refuse (§7 'Deep links'); Go builds and TypeScript reads, so both tests read it. Test data, not a format.
- **Prototype-mark fixtures**: `schema/fixtures/prototype-mark/cases.json` holds raw mark values with their validity and size, and body fragments with the marks a parser must report outside `<template>` and the `doan check` codes they produce (convention 14). Go's parser and check, the TypeScript mark parser and the injected prototype script all read it. Test data, not a format: no schema and no `v`.
- **Design preset fixtures**: `schema/fixtures/cli-envelope/design-skill/` holds envelopes both languages read, not a schema, because the result shape is not a schema (§12).
- **Device-local files without shared schemas**: everything else in userData (`folders.json`, `auto-folders.json`, `onboarding.json`, `preferences.json`, `agent-setup.json`, `ui-state.json`, `canvas-seen.json`, `mcp-connectors.json`, `mcp-connectors-pending.json`, `star-prompt.json`, `thumbnails/`, `conversations/`, `agent-auth/`, `agent-runtimes/`, `terminal/`, `usage-probe/`, `attachments/`; owners in §7) is app-owned, validated internally by TypeScript, never shared with the CLI and never committed. The project-side read-state records in `.doan/seen/` are also TS-only and have no shared schema (§7 'Seen canvases').
- **Canvas row menu and prototype window Export** (Move to Trash, Export): add no userData file, schema, `v` or fixture. The Trash belongs to the OS, the app remembers no export folder, the `canvas:*` and `prototype:*` replies are app-internal, and `doan export`'s `result` has no schema (§12).

## 12. CLI output contract

The public contract for external agents. Envelope: `schema/cli-envelope.v1.schema.json`; usage: `doan help`; what an agent does on each code: the guide `cli/internal/cli/skill.md` (§6).

- Every command has `--json`. Success `{v: 1, ok: true, result}`; failure `{v: 1, ok: false, error: {code, message, nextSteps}}`, where `nextSteps` tells the agent what to do next.
- `error.code` is a snake_case pattern, not an enum, so new codes need no new `v`.
- `error.config` (optional): when a repository command fails on a config file — `repo.Find`'s config stage could not stat it (other than missing or `ENOTDIR`), it is not a regular file, or it could not be read or parsed, or its `v` is unsupported — the failure names that file's absolute path, the parent's when discovery stopped at a broken parent config. An explicit exception, like `root`: a failed command has no root to be relative to. Other failures (canvas enumeration, serving scope, the start path, `repo_not_found`, `doan config`'s value checks, `doan init`'s own checks) carry none, and human output never shows it. Being optional, it kept `v` at 1.
- `error.storageReason` (optional, only `"not_git"`): `ls` without a config (`repo_not_found`) in an existing directory that is in no Git repository (a `.git` at or above HOME does not count, §13), so the app can plan the repository Keep out of Git would create before Set Up. Other commands and other `repo_not_found` causes carry none. Being optional, it kept `v` at 1; an older strict reader rejects it, which only a development app pointed at a newer CLI meets.
- Exit codes tell failure kinds apart; `error.code` and `nextSteps` pick the action (exit 2 covers both `usage_error` and `repo_not_found`, which need different actions). The full code → exit table is `ExitCode` in `cli/internal/cli/envelope.go`, with onboarding pinned by `schema/fixtures/cli-envelope/onboarding/mapping.json`.

| Exit | Meaning |
|---|---|
| 0 | Success |
| 1 | `internal_error`: a bug, and any code `ExitCode` does not map, so mistakes are not hidden |
| 2 | Caller-fixable input: `usage_error`, `repo_not_found`, `invalid_config`, `unsupported_version`, `canvas_not_found`, `export_refused`, `export_too_large`, `frame_not_found` and `not_prototype` (`open` and `export` with `--prototype`), `canvas_unreadable` (`open --prototype`), `not_app_conversation` and `root_mismatch` (`doan handoff`), and onboarding request refusals such as `invalid_url` or `inspection_stale` (`destination_exists` too, which `doan export` reuses) |
| 3 | App unavailable: `app_not_running`, `app_protocol_mismatch`, `project_not_open` (also `app_launch_failed`, onboarding `session_stale`) |
| 4 | `doan check` ran and found error-level findings; envelope stays `ok: true` because the findings are in `result` |
| 5 | Onboarding refusals or failed operations the user or the app must resolve, the storage failures `local_not_git` and `local_exclude_failed` (also from `init` and `config`, 'Storage'), `busy` (`doan open` while the app cannot move or play now), and the `doan handoff` refusals the conversation's state decides (`not_running`, `not_canvas_conversation`, `plan_mode`, `handoff_turn`, `already_requested`, `same_canvas`) |

- Exception: `doan work` turns every app failure into `ok: true`, exit 0, with `delivered: false` (§4 'Work signals over app-socket v3'); only its argument and repo errors (exit 2) fail. `ExitCode` is unchanged.
- `app_not_running` `nextSteps` include the sandbox recovery order (open Doan; run `doan` outside the sandbox or ask approval; then Settings… > About). `project_not_open` covers a folder not saved in Doan, including another worktree of a saved repository, except that a terminal `doan work start` or `update` registers such a worktree (§4 'Auto-registration') and so reports `delivered: true` when it finishes in time.

### Commands and `result` shapes

Optional keys are omitted, never `null`; empty lists are `[]`; paths are repo-relative with `/` unless stated. These shapes are the contract (§11); the app's TypeScript readers depend on them. Edge cases live in `cli/internal/cli/` and its tests.

- **`doan init [--token-css <path>] [--storage local|repo [--git-init]]`** → `{initialized, repo, storage, tokenCss?, created, nextSteps}`. App–CLI contract for project setup (PRODUCT §5 item 8 and app item 0.2).
  - Targets `--repo` or cwd with no upward search; a repo nested in another is allowed. A valid config makes it a no-op (`initialized: false`); a broken one fails and is never overwritten.
  - Token CSS must be a regular file inside the repo, stored as real repo-relative on-disk spelling. Without `--token-css` it prompts only on a terminal without `--json`.
  - Storage ('Storage' below): `--storage local` writes the exclude pair and no `.gitattributes`, and `created` lists the label `.git/info/exclude`; a plain `init` follows a root that is already `local`. `--storage local` on a set-up checkout and explicit `--storage repo` on a `local` root are `usage_error` pointing to `doan config --storage`; a plain `init` with storage `unknown` fails `local_exclude_failed`.
  - The config is published last, after the `.gitattributes` lines or the exclude pair, because a discoverable config marks initialization complete: an exclude write that fails leaves nothing, and a retry never sets up a repo-mode checkout by accident (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-25 'Init published success before merge setup could fail'). No serialization between initializers and no fsync guarantee (`init.go`).
  - `nextSteps` always points to the `context` design preset; `init` runs no model or preset.
- **`doan config [--token-css <path> | --clear-token-css] [--add-serve <path>]... [--remove-serve <path>]... | --storage local|repo [--git-init]`** → `{repo, tokenCss?, serve, changed, storage, storageReason?}`, plus `written` (labels of the files changed: `.gitattributes`, `.git/info/exclude`) and `guidance` (the switch's consequences for the person, PRODUCT §5 item 0 'Doan files') with `--storage`. No write flags is read-only; unchanged operations keep the file and its mtime (product rules: PRODUCT §5 item 0 'Project Settings').
  - Paths are repo-relative or absolute inside the repo and stored in canonical repo-relative spelling; token CSS must be a regular file; serve additions must exist and may not be the repo root, `.doan` metadata (except `canvases/`, `ds/`, `assets/`) or root `.git`; removal accepts targets that no longer exist.
  - The serve-addition exclusion of root `.git` belongs to `config` only; init, schema and `repo.Scope` do not apply it, so a hand-written entry stays eligible under scope rules.
  - `--storage` cannot be combined with another write flag, so a failed call never applies half a change. A request for the current mode is `changed: false` and writes nothing, even when the exclude file cannot be written.
  - Writes go only to the in-repo config, and with `--storage` also to `.gitattributes` and the exclude file ('Storage'); a linked `.doan` never redirects them. App calls serialize per root; separate CLI writers have no lock (`config.go`, `config_ops.go`).
- **`doan ls`** → `{root, scope, storage, storageReason?, canvases: [{path, id?, title?, width?, frames: [{id, title?, implements?}], error?: {code, message}}], git?}`.
  - `canvases` merges `.doan/canvases/` and `.doan/ds/` in path byte order with no type field; the path identifies the type (§1, §13). `root` is the real absolute root; `scope` is the serving scope from the same `repo.Scope` that backs `css_not_served`.
  - A read error is not an empty list: an unreadable canvas sets the row's `error` (`unreadable_canvas`) (PRODUCT §5 item 9). `message` never contains an absolute path. An unparsable but readable canvas has only `path` and `frames: []`, with no `error`. Whole-set failures fail the command.
  - `storage` and `storageReason` as in 'Storage' below.
  - Optional `git`: `{common, main?, branch?, detached?, worktrees}`, absolute canonical paths (an explicit exception, like `root`); `branch` and `detached` are exclusive; missing or broken metadata omits it without failing (§13 'Read-only Git metadata').
- **`doan active`** → `{repo, selection?}`, `selection` `{canvas: {path, id?}, frame?: {id, title?}, element?: {doanId?, selector, html, truncated?, text?}}`, omitted when nothing is selected or another project owns it (§4).
- **`doan check`** → `{findings, summary: {canvases, errors, warnings}}` (§1). `canvases` counts every inspected file, unparsable ones included; zero adds the `no_canvases` warning without failing.
- **`doan work start <canvas path>`** and **`doan work update <canvas path> [--frame <id>] [--note <text>]`** → `{delivered}`; **`doan work done [<canvas path>...]`** → `{delivered, links, skipped}`. `delivered` is true only when the app accepted the v3 request (§4). `links` are `canvasLink`s for the valid paths in argument order, returned whatever `delivered` says; `skipped` lists invalid `done` arguments verbatim, and the done signal is still sent. No paths gives empty lists. A bad `start` or `update` path, and an `update` argument outside §4 'Update arguments', is `usage_error`.
- **`doan onboarding <start | status | check | sign-in | cancel | project inspect | project select | project clone | complete>`** → the v2 reply's `result` (§4). `project inspect`, `select` and `clone` take `--storage local|repo` and send `storage` only when it is given (§4 'Storage in onboarding'). No `--repo` or upward search; paths are absolute; only `start` may launch the app among the onboarding commands (`open` may too).
- **`doan open <canvas path> [--frame <id>]`** → `{opened, frame}` with `opened: true` and `frame` one of `found` (highlighted), `missing` (a frame was asked for but not found, only the canvas opened) or `none` (no frame asked). `found` and `missing` come from the canvas's static frame list, an approximation (§7 'Deep links'). Failures: `usage_error` and `canvas_not_found` for a missing file (2), `project_not_open` (3), `canvas_not_found` for a canvas the app does not list (2), `busy` (5, the reason in `message`), `app_launch_failed`, `app_not_running` and `app_protocol_mismatch` (3). The app being unreachable, not ready or busy is never a success. Rules and the bootstrap: §4.
  - **`--frame <id> --prototype`** → `{opened: true, frame: "found", prototype: true}`: the app started playing that frame in the prototype window and the main window did not move. `--prototype` without `--frame` is `usage_error`. Before the app: `canvas_unreadable` (over 50 MiB, unreadable or unparsable; `nextSteps`: run `doan check`), `frame_not_found` (no such frame in the file, its ID shared, or over 200 UTF-16 units) and `not_prototype` (no valid mark; `nextSteps`: tell the person, mark it only if asked), all exit 2. From the app: `canvas_not_found` for a canvas it does not list or whose canvas ID two rows share (2, `nextSteps` adds `doan check`), `busy` only for first run and the buddy screen (5), and the app-unavailable codes as above. Rules: §4 'Playing a prototype over app-socket v6'.
- **`doan handoff <canvas path> --message <text|->`** → `{requested, canvas}` with `requested: true` and `canvas` the repo-relative path asked for; success means the app took the request, not that a conversation started (that is decided when the turn ends). Failures: `usage_error` (arguments, text, path, an over-long line), `not_app_conversation` (no valid `DOAN_HANDOFF`, or a key the app does not know), `canvas_not_found` for a missing file and `root_mismatch` (2); `app_not_running`, `app_protocol_mismatch` and `project_not_open` (3); `not_running`, `not_canvas_conversation`, `plan_mode`, `handoff_turn`, `already_requested` and `same_canvas` (5); a v5 `bad_request` is `internal_error` (1). It never launches the app, and `doan help` marks it as for Doan app conversations only. Rules: §4.
- **`doan export <canvas path> [--frame <id> --prototype] -o <file> [--force]`** → `{path, output, bytes, frames, external, links}`, plus `prototype: true` with `--frame <id> --prototype` (the key is absent otherwise): `path` is the canvas as `ls` lists it, `output` the absolute file written (an explicit exception, like `root`), `bytes` its size, `frames` the canvas's static frame count, `external` the internet URLs the file still loads, as written (a `//host/x` URL is listed so but loads as `https:` in the file), and `links` the local `<a href>`, `<form action>` and other non-asset references left as written, both sorted and without duplicates. Shape pinned by `cli/internal/cli/export_test.go`; the pipeline is §3 'Exported canvas and viewer'.
  - The canvas argument must be a listed canvas (repo-relative, or absolute inside the repo; case and Unicode spelling map to the listed one), else `canvas_not_found`; a relative `-o` is read from the current directory. An output in any directory named `.doan` (any repo, any depth, also through a link) is a `usage_error`, because a file there is listed as a canvas.
  - `--frame` and `--prototype` come together: either alone is `usage_error`, because a frame alone means nothing to a whole-canvas export and refusing it keeps an agent from believing it exported one frame. The file is the prototype page (§3 'Exported canvas and viewer' 'Prototype page'); the text output is `Exported <path> frame <id> to <output> (<n> bytes)`.
  - Refusals come before any write, in this order, except that a canvas file over 50 MiB is `export_too_large` before it is parsed, and inlining stops at that limit, so only references before the stop are reported: a canvas that does not parse or lacks exactly one positive-integer `doan:width` is `export_refused` with no reference lines; with `--prototype`, the frame judgment of §4 'Playing a prototype over app-socket v6' (`frame_not_found`, `not_prototype`), before inlining so a wrong ID costs no inline; references Doan can't include (SVG `<use>` references that are not fragments, outside the repo or scope, missing direct references, local documents in frames, canvas references under a `<base href>` that moves the folder, custom property `url()`s that resolve to different paths) are `export_refused`; a result over 50 MiB is `export_too_large`; an existing output is `destination_exists` (exit 2, `--force` replaces it, a link at the output is replaced, not followed). `nextSteps` stay inside the guide's approval rules: they never tell an agent to move files or change config on its own.
  - Messages hold no absolute path. `export_refused` and `export_too_large` list one item per line as `- <item>: <detail>` (SVG `<use>` references first, then the rest in document order, `(in <css file>)` after a reference found inside CSS; files largest first, at most five), and the app parses those lines (§7 'Canvas row menu IPC'): an item may contain `": "`, a detail may not.
  - The file is written through a same-directory temporary `.<name>.doan-<hex>` (`O_EXCL`, `O_NOFOLLOW`, 0644 through the umask) and renamed; a failure removes it, and so does a SIGINT or SIGTERM, after which the process exits with 128 + the signal number. A SIGKILL (the app's export timeout in `app/src/main/cli.ts` `runExport` included) can leave it. Only this process's own file is removed, never another export's temporary file of the same name pattern. It reads only repo files, so it needs no app.
- **`doan skill`** → `{version, body}`. `doan help` → `{usage}`.
- **`doan design-skill [<id>]`** reads presets embedded in the CLI; needs no repo, config or app and writes nothing. Without an id → `{version, presets: [{id, title, description, scope, group}]}` in catalog order, never bodies. With an id → `{version, preset, body, sources: [{repository, commit, path}]}`, `body` ending with `[doan:design-skill] end of preset <id> v<version>`. `group` values are in `cli/internal/designskill/catalog.json`; group order is first appearance. Lookup is by catalog key only, never a path; empty, invalid or unknown ids are `usage_error`. Cases: `schema/fixtures/cli-envelope/design-skill/`.

### Storage

Where a Doan root's files are kept (PRODUCT §5 item 0 'Doan files'). Only Go reads and writes it (`cli/internal/repo/storage.go`, `storage_write.go`, `cli/internal/cli/storage.go`); the app reads `storage` from `ls` and `config`.

- **The mode is the exclude pair alone.** `<common dir>/info/exclude` (shared by every worktree of the repository; a submodule's is under the superproject's `.git/modules/<name>/`) holds a marker comment line `# doan: local` directly followed by the root's pattern `/<rel>/.doan/` (`/.doan/` for a root at the Git top level), `rel` relative to the Git top level, escaped byte by byte. gitignore has no end-of-line comments, so the marker cannot share the pattern's line. `.doan/config.json` records no mode, so the two cannot disagree. Lines a person wrote (`.doan`, `.gitignore` entries, `!.doan/`) are neither touched nor read as the mode; a differently cased spelling of the root makes a different pattern (accepted).
- **Values.** `storage` is `local` when the pair is present, `repo` when the exclude was read without it (or is missing, or the folder is not a Git repository), and `unknown` when `.git` or the exclude cannot be read. `storageReason`, omitted when there is none, is `not_git`, `git_unreadable` or `exclude_unwritable` (also a final-file link, a non-regular file, or a file that a pair would push over the 1 MiB cap). Git location comes from `gitLocation`, shared with `ReadGit`, so a `.git` at or above HOME is ignored the same way (§13).
- **Writes.** Adding opens the exclude `O_WRONLY|O_APPEND|O_NOFOLLOW`, creates a missing one exclusively (and `info/` at 0755), adds a newline first when the file does not end with one and keeps its CRLF style, so other writers' lines are never lost. Removing writes a filtered copy to a unique same-directory temporary file with the original's permissions, rechecks the original's identity and bytes right before the rename and refuses on a change, and removes the temporary on failure; every pair for the root is removed. The recheck is not a compare-and-swap against a writer arriving after it (accepted). Reading follows a final-file link as Git does; writing never does. Ancestor directory links are followed (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-24).
- **Order.** `init --storage local` writes the pair before the config; `config --storage repo` adds a missing `.gitattributes` line before removing the pair, so a failure leaves the root `local`; `config --storage local` leaves `.gitattributes` alone (its lines are harmless and the file is the team's).
- **`.gitattributes`.** `.doan/** linguist-documentation` is the only `.gitattributes` line Doan writes, required whenever storage is `repo` (`init` and `config --storage repo` add it when missing; `missingAttributes` in `cli/internal/cli/storage.go`). A `merge=union` line for `.doan/comments/*.jsonl` that earlier builds wrote is left alone, like any other line in the team's file. `init` leaves a set-up repo unchanged (`cli/internal/cli/run_test.go`).
- **Creating a repository** (`--git-init`, `init` and `config` only, with `--storage local`; `cli/internal/repo/gitinit.go`). The decision (`gitInitDecision`) refuses a folder at or above HOME, or with HOME unknown, with a HOME-specific `local_not_git` whose next steps never mention `--git-init`, with or without the flag; `--git-init` without `--storage local` is `usage_error`; a folder already in a repository ignores it. Otherwise, before any other write: git is located as clone does (first `git` on the injected `PATH`; `/usr/bin/git` only when the developer directory from `DEVELOPER_DIR`, `/var/db/xcode_select_link` or the CLT path has `usr/bin/git`; never `xcode-select`), else `git_missing` (exit 5) with nothing written. `<root>/.git` is claimed with an exclusive `mkdir` and its dev/ino recorded; an existing one is `git_init_failed`, left alone. `git init --quiet --template=` then runs in the root with an allow-listed environment (`HOME`, the injected `PATH`, `DEVELOPER_DIR`, `LANG`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`; every other `GIT_*` dropped), stdin closed and stderr discarded, so no hook samples are copied and the person's default branch and object format apply (sha256 repositories read like sha1). A failure is `git_init_failed` (exit 2). After init `ReadStorage` must see the repository; the exclude pair then goes through the ordinary write, which creates the missing `info/`. If any later step fails, the `.git` is removed only when its dev/ino still match the claim; a removal that fails is `cleanup_failed` (exit 5), whose message and next steps name the leftover `.git` path, the one storage message with a path. Only the `.git` is rolled back; the other writes keep their usual failure behavior. `created` and `written` list `.git/`. A kill (the app's `CLI_TIMEOUT_MS`, quit, cancel, Ctrl-C) after init leaves the `.git` and pair without a config; the next setup reads a repository and proceeds (accepted). `PATH` and `DEVELOPER_DIR` reach the command through `Context.Git`, filled from the environment for `init` and `config` only.
- **Errors** (exit 5, the same codes as onboarding): `local_not_git` for local storage in a folder that is not a Git repository (its next steps say to ask the person and pass `--git-init` only when they agree), `local_exclude_failed` for Git metadata or exclude failures when a write is needed, and `git_missing` and `cleanup_failed` from repository creation; `git_init_failed` is exit 2. Messages hold no path or raw I/O error, except `cleanup_failed`'s `.git` path.
- **Labels.** `created`, `written` and onboarding `changes` name the exclude `.git/info/exclude`, a fixed label rather than a path: in a linked worktree or submodule `.git` is a file.
- No Git command runs except that `git init`, and `.git/index` is never read.

### Parsing, output and structure

- **Parsing**: `--json` is detected before parsing, so even a parse failure produces an envelope. Unknown flags are `usage_error`; `--` ends flags. Details in `args.go` and its tests.
- **Output**: with `--json`, stdout is one envelope line and stderr is empty. Without it, failures go to stderr with the same exit code. The exception is export's SIGINT/SIGTERM cleanup (§12 'Commands and `result` shapes'): it exits with 128 + the signal number and no envelope, and may print a cleanup failure to stderr. Human output escapes C0/C1 control characters except LF and HT, because repository text from git, such as canvas titles, may carry terminal control sequences. Non-UTF-8 file bytes become U+FFFD so the envelope still validates.
- **Structure**: `main` only calls `cli.Run(args, stdin, stdout, stderr) int`. Command bodies never touch `os.Exit` (except export's signal cleanup, above), writers, cwd, clock or environment, so tests and callers reuse them (`run.go`).
- (References: Orca's `{ok, result | error: {code, message}}` envelope, verified in its local bundle; open-claude-design's meaningful exit codes, github.com/maxritter/open-claude-design.)

## 13. Multiple repos and worktrees

- The app keeps several repo and worktree roots registered and watched, with one loaded workspace.
- **Discovery** (`cli/internal/repo/find.go`): `--repo` is checked alone and never walks up; otherwise the first directory upward with `.doan/config.json` wins, so the inner repo wins when nested. A broken inner config stops the search instead of falling through to an outer repo. `.git` is never consulted. The app never reads config itself; it uses `doan ls` and `doan config` (Show in Finder only checks that the config path the CLI named for a Config error exists, §7 'Recovery').
- **Paths inside the repo**: every repo path (`tokenCss`, `serve`, `--token-css`, `data-doan-implements`, local CSS links and `@import`s) resolves the same way: lexical check first (a missing `../x` is outside, not missing), then symlinks, then the real path compared with the real root by path segment, never string prefix. Errors that canvas or config content alone can cause are "missing" findings; other I/O errors are `internal_error`. `doan export` and `doan check` resolve through one `repo.Resolver` for the whole command (`cli/internal/repo/resolve.go`): it finds on-disk spelling from an index of each real directory, read once per command and keyed by the real path, never the lexical one, so a file created after that read is missing for the rest of the command (export still opens each file with `O_NOFOLLOW` when it reads it). Read errors are returned and not cached. One-off resolutions (`repo.Resolve`, `repo.Scope`, `tokenCss`) build their own.
- **Listing** (`files.go`): symlinks, and a symlinked `.doan` or canvas directory, are never followed, so nothing is read or written outside the repo. The one exception is the storage exclude file in the Git common directory (§12 'Storage'). The app's one path classifier is `app/src/formats/canvasPath.ts`; filesystem discovery stays in Go.
- The skill tells agents to ask when the instruction's root differs from their working directory (§1).

### The app's folders

Discovery stays in Go only; the app never re-implements listing, discovery or config reading. Product behavior: PRODUCT §5 app items 0–1. Main: `app/src/main/shell.ts`.

- **Opening** runs `doan ls --json` from the picked folder so Go owns subfolder-to-root discovery, and stores `result.root`. **Reading** is always `doan ls --json --repo <stored path>` through the CLI `cliCommand` selects (§7 'CLI lookup and local package'); each folder fails alone.
- **Re-root**: on `repo_not_found` the app reruns `ls` from a hand-added folder and stores a different `root` it finds (an auto-registered root is never re-rooted upward, §4 'Auto-registration'); Set Up Project repeats this right before `doan init`, so the app never creates a nested repo. Success re-reads the stored path and never adopts `result.repo` (POSTMORTEM 2026-10-21).
- **Watching** (`watch.ts`, `designChange.ts`): symlinked targets are not watched, matching the CLI's discovery. Served `scope` entries are watched through their parent directory, so atomic-rename saves and wiped build outputs are seen (limit: a nested served directory missing during a read loses its parent watcher until the next read). A canvas HTML name moves only its row's `rev`; any other or unknown name, or a served change, bumps `servedRev`. Atomic-write temporaries beside a changed canvas are ignored because agent editors create and rename them within one debounce. Settings and other metadata under `.doan/` never count as served changes. `.doan/seen/` has its own non-recursive watcher whose change only re-reads that root's read-state records: no `doan ls`, served bump or canvas reload.
- **Canvas view updates** (`app/src/renderer/canvasView.ts`): the open canvas reloads only on `servedRev` or its own row's `rev` (§3 'Moved canvas', §3 'Accepted reload risk').
- **Row errors**: an `ls` row with `error` stays in the list, so consumers check the error first.
- **Home metadata**: `git` on list entries is the CLI's optional `ls` `git` result; `mtimeMs` and `changedAt` are app display fields, not CLI output. `.git/HEAD` is not watched; roots are reread on window focus. Removing a folder never touches files on disk.

### Read-only Git metadata

Go interprets Git metadata for the optional `ls` `git` result and storage (§12); TypeScript tests `.git` presence during Add and reads remotes from `.git/config` for onboarding's project inspection (§7 'First-run onboarding'). The Git commands Doan runs are onboarding's clone (§7) and Keep out of Git's `git init` (§12 'Storage'); the Git metadata it writes is the storage exclude pair and the files `git init` makes. TypeScript still finds no ancestor `.git` itself: the app reads `not_git` from `ls`. Algorithm: `cli/internal/repo/git.go`.

- A `.git` marker at or above the canonical home directory is ignored, so a dotfiles repository cannot group unrelated projects. An invalid nearest marker never falls through to an outer repository.
- Metadata files must be regular and small and registrations are capped; broken metadata omits `git` without failing `ls`. The cap is not a total I/O or latency bound, and path text with a symlink inside a `..` span can parse differently from Git.

## 14. Agent evaluation

Development tooling for [USABILITY-EVAL.md](./USABILITY-EVAL.md), run by `/agent-eval`; not shipped. It measures how real Claude Code and Codex use Doan through the shell CLI. Runner `eval/run.ts` and `eval/lib/`, with no package manifest of its own (socket validation loads the app's installed Ajv); scenarios `eval/scenarios/<id>/` (format and validation in `eval/lib/scenario.ts`; each scenario's intent in its `rubric.md` and `checks.ts`). Results go to `.scratch/agent-eval/<round>/`; what matters is copied into USABILITY-EVAL.md.

- **Isolation** (`eval/lib/workspace.ts`): each run gets a temp directory under `/tmp` (outside any checkout, short for the socket-path limit) with the seed repo, its own `doan` first on PATH and its own runtime. The real stub bytes and the Codex rule from the app's `codexRuleText()` (imported, never copied) join the seed in one baseline commit, so none counts as agent output. Nothing is installed in the real home; real user state (`app/runtime/real-state.ts`) is compared before and after every round. Fake apps (`eval/lib/socket.ts`) answer the socket with an explicit `DOAN_RUNTIME`, so nothing launches; every eval test that runs the CLI also sets an absolute temporary `DOAN_RUNTIME` (POSTMORTEM 2026-11-02). Agents and judges get no `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CODEX_THREAD_ID`, `CODEX_SESSION_ID` or `DOAN_HANDOFF` from the runner (`eval/lib/agent.ts`), so each agent's work signals carry its own session and a runner started inside a Doan conversation passes no key; the fake-agent fixture runner injects a fixed one. The Claude allow rules go into the run's project `.claude/settings.json` from the app's `CLAUDE_ALLOW_RULES` (imported); under `-p` with Bash in `--allowedTools` they cannot show whether a permission prompt is avoided. `shellCli: false` is PATH setup, not an access-control boundary. A `git: false` scenario (`scenario.json`, O5) runs the agent in a folder outside any Git repository: the baseline commit lives in a bare `seed.git` beside it that the runner's Git reads name explicitly (`--git-dir`), and Codex gets `--skip-git-repo-check` because `exec` refuses an untrusted non-Git folder; its sandbox and approval settings are unchanged.
- **CLI**: real rounds require the packaged CLI so agents see what users get; the self-test builds its own.
- **Agent commands** (`eval/lib/agent.ts`, pinned): Claude `stream-json` keeps tool calls in the transcript. Codex runs sandboxed with approvals off, relying on the Codex rule for the app socket, with shell snapshots off and in a non-login shell (`allow_login_shell=false`) so neither a cached environment nor the user's profile can put an installed `doan` ahead of the run-local PATH. Claude ignores every MCP server; Codex can only disable a leftover `doan` server by name, and the runner adds that override only when `codex mcp list --json` reports one, because Codex 0.166 refuses to start on an override for a server it does not define (an unreadable list stops the run). Runs of one agent are sequential.
- **Agent CLI provenance**: each prompt asks the agent to report `command -v doan`; the automatic `agent_cli_provenance` check fails a run whose answer is missing or differs, even when scenario checks pass. It verifies the initial lookup only. (Reference: OpenAI security cookbook on shell snapshots, https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/enterprise/security-cookbook.)
- **Check contract** (`eval/lib/types.ts`): `checks.ts` default-exports `(ctx) => Check[]`; a throw or an empty or malformed list is a failed `checks_error`. Checks read and run only through `ctx`, and paths leaving the repo throw. `ctx.work()` returns the fake app's v3 exchanges (`work.jsonl`, token-free) with the SHA-256 of each target canvas when the request arrived (null when missing), so `eval/lib/work.ts` judges start-before-edit (an `update` counts as `start`) and done-at-end by content, not by prose; an accepted `update` also carries the target canvas's text when it arrived, so W5 requires, for each requested frame, an `update` naming it while that frame still had its seed content, and fails a frame edited without an `update` naming it since the last observed edit; `ctx.open()` does the same for v4 (`eval/lib/open.ts`: W4 requires `doan open` with the canvas and frame and an accepted request after the final edit, and W3b, W5, W7, R8d, R9b, R10 and R11 require that no `doan open` ran and no v4 or v6 request arrived); `ctx.prototype()` returns the v6 exchanges (`prototype.jsonl`, token-free) with the target canvas's hash when an accepted request arrived, which the fake app answers in the app's order as far as a fake can reach it (`bad_request`, `bad_token`, `project_not_open`, `canvas_not_found`, else `played`; it has no loading project, first run or buddy screen, so never `not_ready` or `busy`, and renders no window), so W8 requires `doan open --frame <id> --prototype` and an accepted v6 request for that canvas and frame whose hash matches the final canvas (`eval/lib/prototype.ts` reads the command evidence; canvas paths compare after `realpath`, relative ones from the repository root as the CLI's `workTarget` does); `ctx.handoff()` returns the v5 exchanges (`handoff.jsonl`, token-free) with the target canvas's hash when an accepted request arrived (`eval/lib/handoff.ts`: R10 requires exactly one accepted request, for the one new canvas, written before the request, with a non-empty message; R11 (`handoffsForCanvases`) exactly one accepted request for each of its two new canvases, each written before its request and with a non-empty message, where only `handoff_each_canvas_once` judges count and paths and the other two look only at accepted requests, so one missing handoff fails one check; and R8d and R9b that no `doan handoff` ran and no v5 request arrived). Agent behavior is judged from invocation evidence (`ctx.calls`, `eval/lib/transcript.ts`), never from guide or tool-result prose, and invocation alone does not prove success. Checker subprocesses get narrow protections (`python -I`, Git hooks and filters off; `eval/lib/context.ts`), not a sandbox.
- **Handoff scenarios** (`scenario.json` `handoff: {canvas}`, `eval/lib/scenario.ts`): the run stands in for a Doan canvas conversation on that canvas. The agent's environment gets a fresh `DOAN_HANDOFF`, and the fake app answers v5 for that key in the app's check order as far as a fake can reach it (`bad_request`, `bad_token`, `project_not_open`, `not_app_conversation`, `already_requested` for a path the run already had accepted, `same_canvas`; a refusal reserves no path); without the setting it answers v5 as an older app does, with v1 `bad_request`. The stand-in sets only `DOAN_HANDOFF`, not `DOAN_APP_CONVERSATION` or `DOAN_CONVERSATION_ID`, so `doan work start` and `done` are delivered there, unlike in a real app conversation (§4 'Suppression'): these scenarios measure the handoff request, not the rest of the conversation environment. The workspace carries only the terminal Codex rule (`codexRuleText()`), not the profile's `doan-handoff.rules`, and Codex runs with approvals off, so the permission path of a real Doan Codex conversation (§7 'Agent authentication' 'Profiles') is checked in the real app, not here.
- **Judge** (`eval/lib/judge.ts`): the other model family scores `rubric.md` (standing exception, AGENTS.md 'Runtime differences'); `--no-judge` skips it. Output `{ verdict: pass|partial|fail, reasons, stalls, layer: guide|output|check|product|none }`; an unparseable answer is `judge_error` and the round continues.
- **Result**: any failed check → `fail`; otherwise the judge's verdict. The judge never turns a failed check into a pass. Known conditions (`eval/lib/known-conditions.ts`) and the summary's classification only label runs in `summary.md` (`eval/lib/record.ts`); they never change results.
- **Self-test**: `node eval/run.ts --self-test` runs every scenario's fixtures with a fake agent and judge; the pass fixture must pass every check and the fail fixture must fail at least one of the scenario's own checks. `<kind>.before.commands.json` runs before the fixture's files are overlaid and `<kind>.commands.json` after, so commands can bracket the edit; a pass fixture's failing command is reported in `problems`.
- **Scenarios are fixed after their first recorded round** so rounds stay comparable; a change to task, seed or intent gets a new id (`W1b`). A checker fix that only corrects false results, or a rubric clarification of existing intent, may land in place with a note on the affected round. A superseded scenario is removed from `eval/scenarios/`; its rows stay in USABILITY-EVAL.md. The current design-preset set is D1, D2b, D4b, D5, D6, D7b, D8, D9, D10g, D10h, D11d, D11e, D12 and D13. Prompts that mirror app text are compared with the app function in tests, not copied: R8d, R9b, R10 and R11 with `turnPrompt` (`eval/lib/turn-prompt.ts`), as a canvas conversation's turn that may hand off. `eval/lib/answer.ts` holds `finalAnswer` (the final answer from a Claude or Codex transcript) and `withoutCode` (fences and inline code removed); H5 and R9b use `finalAnswer`, and R9b also `withoutCode`. R9b reads reply links with the app's `parseReplyAnchor`.
